Excellent. Now I have comprehensive understanding. Let me compile the findings in a clear report:

## Analysis: Why Leaders Are 1.6-2.4x Faster on AMD MI355X Kernel Competitions

Based on thorough analysis of your skills, submissions, and reference implementations, here's what the leaderboard leaders are doing differently, and what's actually possible within your constraints.

---

## The Performance Gap: Three Kernels Analyzed

| Kernel | Your Best | Leader | Gap | Bottleneck |
|--------|----------|--------|-----|-----------|
| **GEMM** | 13.4µs | 4.3µs | **3.1x** | Quantization dispatch (26-84µs) exceeds GEMM compute (7-10µs) |
| **MoE** | 154.2µs | 109.8µs | **1.4x** | All Python API paths exhausted |
| **MLA** | 69.7µs | 33.0µs | **2.1x** | Python dispatch floor (~20-25µs) per torch op |

---

## What Leaders Are Doing (Most Likely)

### 1. **Custom Fused HIP/ASM Kernels (Not Python APIs)**

The evidence is clear: your submissions use `aiter.gemm_a4w4` (Python-wrapped ASM), `aiter.fused_moe`, and `aiter.mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd` — all of which have dispatch overhead.

**Leaders likely:**
- **Write custom HIP kernels that fuse operations:** For GEMM, a single kernel does `quant_mxfp4_dequant_multiply_add` in one launch instead of three (`dynamic_mxfp4_quant` → `e8m0_shuffle` → `gemm_a4w4`)
- **Use CK-Tile or hand-tuned ASM directly** (not via Python API wrappers)
- **For MoE:** Fuse `moe_sorting` + `stage1` + `stage2` into a single persistent kernel with no Python orchestration between stages
- **For MLA:** Write a single fused attention kernel (Q@K^T + softmax + @V) with zero Python dispatch overhead

### 2. **Hardware-Native Primitives (gfx950 MFMA Instructions)**

Your submission skills mention:
- `mfma_f32_32x32x64_f8f6f4` (native MXFP4 MFMA instruction on gfx950)
- `mfma_f32_32x32x64_f8f6f4_scale_mfma` for scaled MXFP4

Leaders likely:
- **Use CK-Tile's gfx950 native MXFP4 instructions** (from `composable_kernel/example/ck_tile/18_flatmm/`)
- **Avoid any fp4 re-quantization between stages** by packing the layout to match MFMA lane ordering directly
- **Use wave64 DPP instructions** for expert routing in MoE (vs Python `torch.topk`)

### 3. **No Python Dispatch Between Operations**

Your MLA submission uses 2-3 separate torch ops with ~20-25µs dispatch each. The 2.1x gap is almost entirely Python overhead.

Leaders:
- **Single kernel launch = zero re-enter cost**
- **For MLA specifically:** Fused CK/HIP attention kernel that handles K≠V head dimensions (576 for K, 512 for V) natively

---

## What's Actually Possible Within Popcorn CLI's Single-File Constraint

Your skills confirm: **popcorn-cli ONLY uploads `submission.py`.** No multi-file modules. No HIP compilation on runner (static source scanning blocks `hipModuleLaunchKernel`, `<<<>>>`, `load_inline`).

**Viable paths within constraints:**

### Path 1: Custom Triton JIT Kernels (PARTIALLY VIABLE)
- ✅ **Can write:** Inline Triton kernels with `@triton.jit` decorator
- ❌ **Cannot use:** `tl.dot_scaled` with fp4 (KeyError on runner — `float4_e2m1fn_x2` missing from type registry)
- ⚠️ **Limitation:** Triton kernels are ~68% slower than hand-tuned ASM (you've confirmed this)

### Path 2: Pre-compiled Kernel Objects (POTENTIALLY VIABLE IF NOT BLOCKED)
- ✅ **Can import:** Pre-compiled `.so` or `.co` objects if they're installed on the runner
- ❌ **Cannot create:** Custom HIP compilation from submission.py (blocked)
- ⚠️ **Unknown:** Whether custom `.so` imports are blocked by static source scanning

### Path 3: Algorithmic Tricks Within Existing APIs (EXHAUSTED)
- Your skills show 18+ phases of experimentation on MoE, 17+ on MLA, 16+ on GEMM
- Parameter tuning is confirmed dead (KSPLIT, BYPASS, block_m all exhausted)
- `torch.compile` is dead (ROCm 7.1 `auto_functionalized_v2` blocks aiter ops)
- **This path yields at most 5-10% improvement** (you've hit diminishing returns)

### Path 4: HipKittens-Based Custom Kernels (MENTIONED BUT SEEMINGLY BLOCKED)
Your skill `amd-speedrun-research-baseline` section 1.0.0 says:
> "HipKittens — BLOCKED ON RUNNER. Requires hipcc AOT compilation → .so → pybind11 import. Links against libamdhip64.so (explicitly blocked by runner scanner)."

**This is a dead end given the constraints.**

---

## Technically Possible But Constraint-Blocked Approaches

### 1. **Fused Quant+GEMM HIP Kernel (HIGHEST POTENTIAL IF ALLOWED)**

Your `amd-gemm-mxfp4-optimization` skill documents this as the best ever improvement:
> "Before: dynamic_mxfp4_quant (26µs) → e8m0_shuffle (1-3µs) → gemm_a4w4 (7-10µs)"
> "After: fused_quant_shuffle (23µs) → gemm_a4w4 (7-10µs) — 23.8% improvement"

**Why it works:** Single kernel = one dispatch, fused memory layout = better VGPR reuse.
**Why blocked:** Runner static scanner blocks all HIP compilation patterns.

### 2. **Direct CK-Tile Dispatch via ctypes/pybind11 (UNKNOWN IF POSSIBLE)**

CK-Tile kernels are pre-compiled (e.g., `/home/runner/aiter/hsa//gfx950/`). The question: **Can you call them directly without going through aiter's Python wrapper?**

Your submission shows aiter DOES this internally. Whether you can replicate it from submission.py is unknown — depends on whether the runner's source scanner blocks ctypes FFI calls.

### 3. **Expert Masking in MoE (PARTIALLY TESTED)**

Your `amd-moe-mxfp4-optimization` skill mentions:
> "Key Discovery: moe_sorting_fwd has native expert masking via local_expert_mask parameter (March 2026)"
> "Untested path: Pass expert_mask = (bincount > 0).to(torch.int32) as local_expert_mask. This may skip sorting overhead for ~200 empty experts."

This is **untested and potentially high-impact** (could save 10-15µs if it skips empty expert loops).

---

## The Hard Ceiling: Why 3.1x on GEMM Is So Hard

From your `amd-gemm-mxfp4-optimization` skill:

```
Leader: 4.3µs (likely a single fused quant+GEMM kernel)
Your best: 13.4µs = ~26µs quant + 7-10µs GEMM

At Python level, you're at the ceiling. The ONLY way to beat it:
- Fuse quantization into the GEMM kernel (requires custom HIP/CK-Tile)
- Or use a pre-quantized activation path (task generates bf16, so infeasible)
```

**The math:** Even if you eliminate ALL Python dispatch overhead, you still need 7-10µs for GEMM + 26-84µs for quant = 33-94µs minimum. Leader is 4.3µs, so the kernel itself must be fused and run at 4.3µs total, which means quant overhead is literally burned into the GEMM compute.

---

## What You Can Actually Try (Within Constraints)

### 1. **AITER Unexplored Kernels (LOW RISK, MEDIUM REWARD)**

From `amd-speedrun-research-baseline`:
```
AITER kernels not tried:
- asm_moe() — claims "best performance on AMD"
- fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1() — block-scaled FP8, claims 3x
- ck_moe_2stages() — independent quant per stage
- fused_moe_dp_share_expert() — DeepSeek-specific routing
- blockPerCu in CKTile (PR #2313) — 1-4 blocks per CU
- _moe_mxfp4_sort_kernel optimization (PR #2414) — 30-52% sort speedup
```

**Action:** Probe each kernel's signature on runner to see if they accept MXFP4 inputs and are callable from submission.py.

### 2. **Expert Masking for MoE (UNTESTED, POTENTIALLY 10-15µs GAIN)**

Your skill documents the `local_expert_mask` parameter. Try:
```python
expert_mask = (torch.bincount(topk_ids.flatten(), minlength=num_experts) > 0).to(torch.int32)
return fused_moe(..., expert_mask=expert_mask)
